She initially poured herself into art‑therapy training plans, meticulous household budgeting, and her garden, but gradually her routines became less grounding and more rigid, and she withdrew from her book club and local arts events she once loved. Her family noticed that she stopped participating in shared activities, let household tasks lapse despite being at home, and spent hours isolated in the rose garden or at her laptop without finishing projects she had started, which led to growing tension and arguments with her spouse about her apparent loss of motivation and engagement. After an especially strained family conversation where she admitted that even painting and cooking no longer felt meaningful, her spouse encouraged her to see a psychiatrist to understand why she no longer cared about the activities and relationships that once defined her days.\n- Chief Complaint: “I don’t feel any interest in the things I used to love—my art, my garden, even time with my family just feels flat.”\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S019 (Delusions): I keep coming back to this idea that my husband’s work trips and the kids pulling away aren’t normal changes, but part of a plan to push me out of the family so they can watch how I react, and no matter how many times they tell me that’s not true, it still feels like there’s some test going on that I’m failing.\n- S020 (Hallucinations): Sometimes when I’m alone in the house, especially late at night, I hear a man and a woman whispering my name from the hallway or the garden, commenting on how I’m doing as a mother, even though I know there’s no one actually there.\n- S021 (Disorganized_Thinking_Speech): When I try to explain what’s going on in my head, I start talking about the garden, then jump to money, then to my kids’ future, and halfway through I forget what you even asked me because my thoughts just slide off in different directions.\n- S024 (Depressed_Mood): Most days I wake up with this heavy, sinking feeling, like there’s a gray film over everything, and even when nothing particularly bad is happening I just feel sad and used up for no clear reason.\n- S025 (Loss_of_Interest): I used to lose hours happily painting or planning out new plants for the yard, and now I just sit there staring at the canvas or the seed packets, feeling nothing and wondering why I should bother when it all seems pointless.\n- S026 (Weight_Appetite_Change): My eating has been all over the place—some weeks I barely feel hungry and skip meals without noticing, then other weeks I stand in the kitchen at night mindlessly snacking even though I’m not really tasting the food, and my clothes have started fitting differently because of it.\n- S030 (Worthlessness_Guilt): I walk around with this constant feeling that I’ve ruined my kids’ lives by checking out emotionally and that every argument or bad grade is somehow my fault, like I’m a burden they’d all be better off without.\n- S032 (Death_Suicide): On my worst days I catch myself thinking it wouldn’t be so bad if I just didn’t wake up, and I’ve gone as far as picturing driving my car into a tree, but then I get scared and push the thought away because I don’t actually want to do that to my family.\n- S033 (Elevated_Irritable_Mood): There are stretches where I suddenly feel wired and almost giddy for no good reason, talking fast and getting annoyed at everyone over tiny things, like the dishwasher not being loaded my way, and I can’t seem to dial it back.\n- S034 (Increased_Activity): When I get into those high-energy phases, I rush around the house starting a dozen projects—reorganizing closets, redesigning the garden layout, planning online art courses—all at once, and then crash later with half-finished piles everywhere.\n- S035 (Inflated_Self_Esteem): In those up periods, I get this strong conviction that I’m meant to create some huge, important art-therapy program that will change how people heal, and I feel almost embarrassed later when I look back at how certain I was.\n- S036 (Decreased_Sleep): During the more ‘up’ times I can go on three or four hours of sleep, spending half the night on the laptop planning things or rearranging the house, and I still wake up buzzing and not the least bit tired.\n- S038 (Racing_Thoughts): My mind can feel like a radio stuck between stations, with ideas about money, the kids, paint colors, and spiritual stuff all blasting at once so fast that I can’t grab onto any single thought long enough to finish it.\n- S039 (Manic_Distractibility): When I’m in that keyed-up mode, I’ll be paying bills and then a bird outside or a notification on my phone pulls me away, and before I know it I’m watering plants or looking up something random online, leaving everything half done.\n- S040 (Increased_Goal_Activity): In those higher-energy spells I start emailing old friends, signing up for workshops, planning big family outings, and even initiating sex more than usual, but I rarely follow through and end up feeling embarrassed and exhausted afterward.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- psychotic_symptoms_and_manic_episode_occur_concurrently: Some of the times when I’ve felt the most wired and slept only a few hours, racing around starting projects, were also when the whispers about my parenting were the loudest and I was most convinced my family was secretly testing me.\n- manic_episode_is_present_for_the_majority_of_the_total_duration_of_the_active_and_residual_portions_of_the_illness: Looking back over the last couple of years, those sped‑up, overconfident phases where I’m buzzing with ideas and starting big plans have taken up more of the time than the really low stretches, even if they come and go.\n- delusions_and_hallucinations_occur_for_2_or_more_weeks_in_the_absence_of_a_major_mood_episode: There was a stretch last year when my mood felt more or less neutral, but I still heard the nighttime whispering and was sure my husband’s trips were part of some experiment on me, even though I wasn’t particularly high or low emotionally.\n- functional_impairment: I’ve let bills pile up, stopped going to my book club and art events, and even at home I miss appointments and forget basic chores, so my relationships feel strained and I don’t feel capable of holding a job or finishing the projects I start.\n- duration: All of this—the flattening of my interest, the mood swings, and the strange experiences—has been building and sticking around for a little over two years, ever since my husband started traveling more and the kids became more independent.\n- psychotic_symptoms_duration: The whispers and the fixed idea that my family is somehow observing or testing me have been happening on and off for roughly the last year and a half.\n- manic_episode_duration: Those wired, fast‑talking, little‑sleep phases with big plans have been coming and going for close to a year and eight months now.\n- depressive_episode_duration: The heavy, empty, guilty stretches where everything feels pointless have been a problem for about seven months in total over this period.\n\nRules:\n- She initially poured herself into art‑therapy training plans, meticulous household budgeting, and her garden, but gradually her routines became less grounding and more rigid, and she withdrew from her book club and local arts events she once loved. Her family noticed that she stopped participating in shared activities, let household tasks lapse despite being at home, and spent hours isolated in the rose garden or at her laptop without finishing projects she had started, which led to growing tension and arguments with her spouse about her apparent loss of motivation and engagement. After an especially strained family conversation where she admitted that even painting and cooking no longer felt meaningful, her spouse encouraged her to see a psychiatrist to understand why she no longer cared about the activities and relationships that once defined her days.\n- Chief Complaint: “I don’t feel any interest in the things I used to love—my art, my garden, even time with my family just feels flat.”\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S019 (Delusions): I keep coming back to this idea that my husband’s work trips and the kids pulling away aren’t normal changes, but part of a plan to push me out of the family so they can watch how I react, and no matter how many times they tell me that’s not true, it still feels like there’s some test going on that I’m failing.\n- it all seems pointless.\n- S026 (Weight_Appetite_Change): My eating has been all over the place—some weeks I barely feel hungry and skip meals without noticing, then other weeks I stand in the kitchen at night mindlessly snacking even though I’m not really tasting the food, and my clothes have started fitting differently because of it.\n- S030 (Worthlessness_Guilt): I walk around with this constant feeling that I’ve ruined my kids’ lives by checking out emotionally and that every argument or bad grade is somehow my fault, like I’m a burden they’d all be better off without.\n- S032 (Death_Suicide): On my worst days I catch myself thinking it wouldn’t be so bad if I just didn’t wake up, and I’ve gone as far as picturing driving my car into a tree, but then I get scared and push the thought away because I don’t actually want to do that to my family.\n- S033 (Elevated_Irritable_Mood): There are stretches where I suddenly feel wired and almost giddy for no good reason, talking fast and getting annoyed at everyone over tiny things, like the dishwasher not being loaded my way, and I can’t seem to dial it back.\n- S034 (Increased_Activity): When I get into those high-energy phases, I rush around the house starting a dozen projects—reorganizing closets, redesigning the garden layout, planning online art courses—all at once, and then crash later with half-finished piles everywhere.\n- S035 (Inflated_Self_Esteem): In those up periods, I get this strong conviction that I’m meant to create some huge, important art-therapy program that will change how people heal, and I feel almost embarrassed later when I look back at how certain I was.\n- S036 (Decreased_Sleep): During the more ‘up’ times I can go on three or four hours of sleep, spending half the night on the laptop planning things or rearranging the house, and I still wake up buzzing and not the least bit tired.\n- S038 (Racing_Thoughts): My mind can feel like a radio stuck between stations, with ideas about money, the kids, paint colors, and spiritual stuff all blasting at once so fast that I can’t grab onto any single thought long enough to finish it.\n- S039 (Manic_Distractibility): When I’m in everything half done.\n- S040 (Increased_Goal_Activity): In those higher-energy spells I start emailing old friends, signing up for workshops, planning big family outings, and even initiating sex more family was secretly testing me.\n- manic_episode_is_present_for_the_majority_of_the_total_duration_of_the_active_and_residual_portions_of_the_illness: Looking back over the last couple of years, those sped‑up, overconfident phases where I’m buzzing with ideas and starting big plans have taken up more of the time than the really low stretches, even if they come and go.\n- delusions_and_hallucinations_occur_for_2_or_more_weeks_in_the_absence_of_a_major_mood_episode: There was a stretch last year when my mood felt more or less neutral, but I still heard the nighttime S020 (Hallucinations): Sometimes when I’m alone in the house, especially late at night, I hear a man and a woman whispering my name from the hallway or the garden, commenting on how I’m doing as a mother, even though I know there’s no one actually there.\n- S021 (Disorganized_Thinking_Speech): When I try to explain what’s going on in my head, I start talking about the garden, then jump to laptop planning things or rearranging the house, and I still wake up buzzing and not the least bit tired.\n- S038 (Racing_Thoughts): My mind can feel like a radio stuck between stations, with ideas S020 (Hallucinations): Sometimes when I’m alone in the house, especially late at night, I hear a man and a woman whispering my name from the hallway or the garden, commenting on how I’m doing as a Her family noticed that she stopped participating in shared activities, let household tasks lapse despite being at home, and spent hours isolated in the rose garden or at her laptop without finishing projects she had started, which led to growing tension and arguments with her spouse about her apparent loss of motivation and engagement. longer cared about the activities and relationships that once defined her days.\n- Chief Complaint: “I don’t feel any interest in the things I used to love—my art, my garden, even time with my family just feels flat.”\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S019 (Delusions): I keep coming back to this idea that my husband’s work trips and the kids pulling away aren’t normal changes, but part of a plan to push me out of the family so they can watch how I react, and no matter how many times they tell me that’s not true, it still feels like there’s some test going on that I’m failing.\n- S020 (Hallucinations): Sometimes when I’m alone in the house, especially late at night, I hear a man and a woman whispering my name from the hallway or the garden, commenting on how I’m doing as a mother, even though I know there’s no one actually there.\n- S021 (Disorganized_Thinking_Speech): When I try to explain what’s going on in my head, I start talking about the garden, then jump to fast and getting annoyed at everyone over tiny things, like the dishwasher not being loaded my way, and I can’t seem to dial it back.\n- S034 (Increased_Activity): When I get into those high-energy phases, I rush around the house starting a dozen projects—reorganizing closets, redesigning the garden layout, planning online art courses—all at once, and then crash later with half-finished piles everywhere.\n- S035 (Inflated_Self_Esteem): In those up periods, I get this strong conviction that I’m meant to create some huge, important art-therapy program that will change how people heal, and I feel almost embarrassed later when I look back at how certain I was.\n- S036 (Decreased_Sleep): During the more ‘up’ times I can go on three or four hours of sleep, spending half the night on the laptop planning things or rearranging the house, and I still wake up buzzing and not the least bit tired.\n- S038 (Racing_Thoughts): My mind can feel like a radio stuck between stations, with ideas about money, the kids, paint colors, and spiritual stuff all blasting at once so fast that I can’t grab onto any single thought long enough to finish it.\n- S039 (Manic_Distractibility): When I’m in everything half done.\n- S040 (Increased_Goal_Activity): In those higher-energy spells I start emailing old friends, signing up for workshops, planning big family outings, and even initiating sex more sped‑up, overconfident phases where I’m buzzing with ideas and starting big plans have taken up more of the time than the really low stretches, even if they come and go.\n- delusions_and_hallucinations_occur_for_2_or_more_weeks_in_the_absence_of_a_major_mood_episode: There was a stretch last year when my mood felt more or less neutral, but I still heard the nighttime When I look back, they actually take up the majority of the time compared to my low stretches, even though they come and go in waves.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced the whispers or the feeling of being tested for a longer period—say, a month or more—during which your mood was completely stable, without feeling wired or down?\n\nPatient: Yes, I've had that happen, though it wasn't quite a month—it was that stretch of about two or three weeks last year. Even though my mood felt completely stable and neutral, I still heard the whispering at night and remained convinced my husband's trips were part of a test.\n\nDoctor: During that two to three week period when your mood was neutral, were the whispers and the belief that you were being tested just as strong as they are during your high or low periods?\n\nPatient: Yes, they were absolutely just as strong and felt completely real to me. Even though my mood wasn't high or low during those weeks, the whispers and the feeling of being tested didn't fade or change at all.\n\nDoctor: When you are not experiencing those high or low mood phases, do the whispers and the feeling of being tested ever go away completely, or do they continue to linger?\n\nPatient: They definitely continue to linger. Even during that stretch last year when my mood was completely neutral, I still heard the whispering at night and remained convinced that my husband's work trips were part of a test on me."

Has this been going on for a few weeks or months, or has it been a constant feeling for several years?\n\nPatient: It’s been building up and sticking around for a little over two years now. It really started when my husband began traveling more for work and the kids became more independent, leaving me alone at home for long stretches. Since then, this flat feeling has just persisted.\n\nDoctor: During these past two years, have you had any periods lasting more than two months where your mood returned to normal and you felt like your usual self?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really had a period where I felt completely back to my usual self. There was a stretch last year where my mood felt relatively neutral, but I was still hearing those whispers and feeling convinced my family was testing me. So even when my mood wasn't high or low, it still didn't feel like a true return to normal.\n\nDoctor: How long did that period last where your mood felt neutral but you were still hearing those whispers and having those thoughts about your family?\n\nPatient: That neutral stretch lasted for about two or three weeks. Even though I wasn't feeling especially high or low during those weeks, the whispering late at night and the feeling that my family was testing me kept happening just the same.\n\nDoctor: When did the whispers and the feeling that your family is testing you first begin? Even though my mood felt completely stable and neutral, I still heard the whispering at night and remained convinced my husband's trips were part of a test.\n\nDoctor: During that two to three week period when your mood was neutral, were the whispers and the belief that you were being tested just as strong as they are during your high or low periods?\n\nPatient: Yes, they were absolutely just as strong and felt completely real to me. Even though my mood wasn't high or low during those weeks, the whispers and the feeling of being tested didn't fade or change at all.\n\nDoctor: When you are not experiencing those high or low mood phases, do the whispers and the feeling of being tested ever go away completely, or do they continue to linger?\n\nPatient: They definitely continue to linger. Even during that stretch last year when my mood was completely neutral, I still heard the whispering at night and remained convinced that my husband's work trips were part of a test on me.\n\nDoctor: Have you or those close to you noticed any times where your speech became jumbled, or where you had significant trouble organizing your thoughts and expressing them clearly to others?\n\nPatient: Yes, that happens a lot when I try to explain what's going on in my head. I might start talking about my garden, but then I'll suddenly jump to money or my kids' future, and halfway through I'll completely forget what you even asked me. It just feels like my thoughts are sliding off in different directions and I can't keep them on track."

The heavy, empty, and down feelings came much later, and have only really been a problem for about seven months in total.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced periods where you felt the exact opposite of down—such as feeling unusually energetic, hyperactive, or needing very little sleep?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do have stretches where I feel completely wired and buzzing with energy, easily getting by on just three or four hours of sleep without feeling tired. During those times, I talk fast, get easily annoyed, and rush around starting a dozen different projects like reorganizing the house or planning art programs.\n\nDoctor: How long do these periods of feeling wired and high-energy typically last, and how often do they occur?\n\nPatient: Those wired phases have been coming and going for close to a year and eight months now. When I look back, they actually take up the majority of the time compared to my low stretches, even though they come and go in waves.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced the whispers or the feeling of being tested for a longer period—say, a month or more—during which your mood was completely stable, without feeling wired or down?\n\nPatient: Yes, I've had that happen, though it wasn't quite a month—it was that stretch of about two or three weeks last year. Even though my mood felt completely stable and neutral, I still heard the whispering at night and remained convinced my husband's trips were part of a test.\n\nDoctor: During that two to three week period when your mood was neutral, were the whispers and the belief that you were being tested just as strong as they are during your high or low periods?\n\nPatient: Yes, they were absolutely just as strong and felt completely real to me. Even though my mood wasn't high or low during those weeks, the whispers and the feeling of being tested didn't fade or change at all.\n\nDoctor: When you are not experiencing those high or low mood phases, do the whispers and the feeling of being tested ever go away completely, or do they continue to linger?\n\nPatient: They definitely continue to linger. Even during that stretch last year when my mood was completely neutral, I still heard the whispering at night and remained convinced that my husband's work trips were part of a test on me.\n\nDoctor: Have you or those close to you noticed any times where your speech became jumbled, or where you had significant trouble organizing your thoughts and expressing them clearly to others?\n\nPatient: Yes, that happens a lot when I try to explain what's going on in my head. I might start talking about my garden, but then I'll suddenly jump to money or my kids' future, and halfway through I'll completely forget what you even asked me. It just feels like my thoughts are sliding off in different directions and I can't keep them on track.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about the whispers you hear? Do they sound like distinct voices talking to each other, or a voice commenting on what you are doing?\n\nPatient: Yes, it sounds like a man and a woman whispering my name from the hallway or the garden late at night. They comment on how I'm doing as a mother, almost like they are judging my parenting. Even though I know there is no one actually there, it still feels incredibly real when it happens."

It just feels like my thoughts are sliding off in different directions and I can't keep them on track.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about the whispers you hear? They comment on how I'm doing as a mother, almost like they are judging my parenting. Even though I know there is no one actually there, it still feels incredibly real when it happens.\n\nDoctor: Have you been using any recreational drugs, alcohol, or new medications, or do you have any medical conditions like thyroid issues that started around the same time as these symptoms?"

S020 (Hallucinations): Sometimes when I’m alone in the house, especially late at night, I hear a man and a woman whispering my name from the hallway or the garden, commenting on how I’m doing as a mother, even though I know there’s no one actually there.\n- S021 (Disorganized_Thinking_Speech): When I try to explain what’s going on in my head, I start talking about the garden, then jump to money, then to my kids’ future, and halfway through I forget what you even asked me because my thoughts just slide off in different directions.\n- S024 (Depressed_Mood): Most days I wake up with this heavy, sinking feeling, like there’s a gray film over everything, and even when nothing particularly bad is happening I just feel sad and used up for no clear reason.\n- S025 (Loss_of_Interest): I used to lose hours happily painting or planning out new plants for the yard, and now I just sit there staring at the canvas or the seed packets, feeling nothing and wondering why I should bother when it all seems pointless.\n- S026 (Weight_Appetite_Change): My eating has been all over the place—some weeks I barely feel hungry and skip meals without noticing, then other weeks I stand in the kitchen at night mindlessly snacking even though I’m not really tasting the food, and my clothes have started fitting differently because of it.\n- S030 (Worthlessness_Guilt): I walk around with this constant feeling that I’ve ruined my kids’ lives by checking out emotionally and that every argument or bad grade is somehow my fault, like I’m a burden they’d all be better off without.\n- S032 (Death_Suicide): On my worst days I catch myself thinking it wouldn’t be so bad if I just didn’t wake up, and I’ve gone as far as picturing driving my car into a tree, but then I get scared and push the thought away because I don’t actually want to do that to my family.\n- S033 (Elevated_Irritable_Mood): There are stretches where I suddenly feel wired and almost giddy for no good reason, talking fast and getting annoyed at everyone over tiny things, like the dishwasher not being loaded my way, and I can’t seem to dial it back.\n- S034 (Increased_Activity): When I get into those high-energy phases, I rush around the house starting a dozen projects—reorganizing closets, redesigning the garden layout, planning online art courses—all at once, and then crash later with half-finished piles everywhere.\n- S035 (Inflated_Self_Esteem): In those up periods, I get this strong conviction that I’m meant to create some huge, important art-therapy program that will change how people heal, and I feel almost embarrassed later when I look back at how certain I was.\n- S036 (Decreased_Sleep): During the more ‘up’ times I can go on three or four hours of sleep, spending half the night on the laptop planning things or rearranging the house, and I still wake up buzzing and not the least bit tired.\n- S038 (Racing_Thoughts): My mind can feel like a radio stuck between stations, with ideas about money, the kids, paint colors, and spiritual stuff all blasting at once so fast that I can’t grab onto any single thought long enough to finish it.\n- S039 (Manic_Distractibility): When I’m in that keyed-up mode, I’ll be paying bills and then a bird outside or a notification on my phone pulls me away, and before I know it I’m watering plants or looking up something random online, leaving everything half done.\n- S040 (Increased_Goal_Activity): In those higher-energy spells I start emailing old friends, signing up for workshops, planning big family outings, and even initiating sex more than usual, but I rarely follow through and end up feeling embarrassed and exhausted afterward.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- psychotic_symptoms_and_manic_episode_occur_concurrently: Some of the times when I’ve felt the most wired and slept only a few hours, racing around starting projects, were also when the whispers about my parenting were the loudest and I was most convinced my family was secretly testing me.\n- manic_episode_is_present_for_the_majority_of_the_total_duration_of_the_active_and_residual_portions_of_the_illness: Looking back over the last couple of years, those sped‑up, overconfident phases where I’m buzzing with ideas and starting big plans have taken up more of the time than the really low stretches, even if they come and go.\n- delusions_and_hallucinations_occur_for_2_or_more_weeks_in_the_absence_of_a_major_mood_episode: There was a stretch last year when my mood felt more or less neutral, but I still heard the nighttime whispering and was sure my husband’s trips were part of some experiment on me, even though I wasn’t particularly high or low emotionally.\n- functional_impairment: I’ve let bills pile up, stopped going to my book club and art events, and even at home I miss appointments and forget basic chores, so my relationships feel strained and I don’t feel capable of holding a job or finishing the projects I start.\n- duration: All of this—the flattening of my interest, the mood swings, and the strange experiences—has been building and sticking around for a little over two years, ever since my husband started traveling more and the kids became more independent.\n- psychotic_symptoms_duration: The whispers and the fixed idea that my family is somehow observing or testing me have been happening on and off for roughly the last year and a half.\n- manic_episode_duration: Those wired, fast‑talking, little‑sleep phases with big plans have been coming and going for close to a year and It's even caused me to withdraw from my life and stop going to my book club.\n\nDoctor: During those times when you feel wired and high-energy, does that feeling typically last continuously for at least a week, or is it usually shorter, lasting only a few days?"

Since then, this flat feeling has just persisted.\n\nDoctor: During these past two years, have you had any periods lasting more than two months where your mood returned to normal and you felt like your usual self?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really had a period where I felt completely back to my usual self. There was a stretch last year where my mood felt relatively neutral, but I was still hearing those whispers and feeling convinced my family was testing me. So even when my mood wasn't high or low, it still didn't feel like a true return to normal.\n\nDoctor: How long did that period last where your mood felt neutral but you were still hearing those whispers and having those thoughts about your family?\n\nPatient: That neutral stretch lasted for about two or three weeks. Even though I wasn't feeling especially high or low during those weeks, the whispering late at night and the feeling that my family was testing me kept happening just the same.\n\nDoctor: When did the whispers and the feeling that your family is testing you first begin? Did they start around the same time you began feeling empty and down, or did one start before the other?\n\nPatient: The whispers and the feeling of being tested actually started first, about a year and a half ago. The heavy, empty, and down feelings came much later, and have only really been a problem for about seven months in total.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced periods where you felt the exact opposite of down—such as feeling unusually energetic, hyperactive, or needing very little sleep?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do have stretches where I feel completely wired and buzzing with energy, easily getting by on just three or four hours of sleep without feeling tired. During those times, I talk fast, get easily annoyed, and rush around starting a dozen different projects like reorganizing the house or planning art programs.\n\nDoctor: How long do these periods of feeling wired and high-energy typically last, and how often do they occur?\n\nPatient: Those wired phases have been coming and going for close to a year and eight months now. When I look back, they actually take up the majority of the time compared to my low stretches, even though they come and go in waves.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced the whispers or the feeling of being tested for a longer period—say, a month or more—during which your mood was completely stable, without feeling wired or down?\n\nPatient: Yes, I've had that happen, though it wasn't quite a month—it was that stretch of about two or three weeks last year. Even though my mood felt completely stable and neutral, I still heard the whispering at night and remained convinced my husband's trips were part of a test.\n\nDoctor: During that two to three week period when your mood was neutral, were the whispers and the belief that you were being tested just as strong as they are during your high or low periods?\n\nPatient: Yes, they were absolutely just as strong and felt completely real to me. Even though my mood wasn't high or low during those weeks, the whispers and the feeling of being tested didn't fade or change at all.\n\nDoctor: When you are not experiencing those high or low mood phases, do the whispers and the feeling of being tested ever go away completely, or do they continue to linger?\n\nPatient: They definitely continue to linger. Even during that stretch last year when my mood was completely neutral, I still heard the whispering at night and remained convinced that my husband's work trips were part of a test on me.\n\nDoctor: Have you or those close to you noticed any times where your speech became jumbled, or where you had significant trouble organizing your thoughts and expressing them clearly to others?\n\nPatient: Yes, that happens a lot when I try to explain what's going on in my head. I might start talking about my garden, but then I'll suddenly jump to money or my kids' future, and halfway through I'll completely forget what you even asked me. It just feels like my thoughts are sliding off in different directions and I can't keep them on track.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about the whispers you hear? Do they sound like distinct voices talking to each other, or a voice commenting on what you are doing?\n\nPatient: Yes, it sounds like a man and a woman whispering my name from the hallway or the garden late at night. They comment on how I'm doing as a mother, almost like they are judging my parenting. Even though I know there is no one actually there, it still feels incredibly real when it happens.\n\nDoctor: Have you been using any recreational drugs, alcohol, or new medications, or do you have any medical conditions like thyroid issues that started around the same time as these symptoms?\n\nPatient: No, I don't use any recreational drugs, and I rarely drink alcohol. I'm not taking any new medications, and as far as I know, I don't have any medical conditions like thyroid issues.\n\nDoctor: When you have those periods of feeling wired and high-energy, how long does a single one of those phases typically last continuously—is it usually a few days, a week, or even longer?\n\nPatient: I'm not entirely sure of the exact number of days or weeks that each individual high-energy phase lasts continuously. I just know that these wired periods have been coming and going for about a year and eight months now.\n\nDoctor: During those times when you felt wired and full of energy, did it ever lead to hospitalization, or cause major disruptions in your daily life and relationships?\n\nPatient: No, I’ve never been hospitalized, but those high-energy periods have caused major disruptions in my life. I start a dozen projects and leave them unfinished, let bills pile up, and miss appointments, which has really strained my relationships with my family. It's even caused me to withdraw from my life and stop going to my book club.\n\nDoctor: During those times when you feel wired and high-energy, does that feeling typically last continuously for at least a week, or is it usually shorter, lasting only a few days?\n\nPatient: I'm not entirely sure of the exact number of days because I tend to lose track of time when I'm in those wired phases. However, they definitely feel like they go on for quite a while, rather than just lasting a few days."
  }

No, I don't experience those two states at the same time; they happen in completely separate phases. I will have periods where I am wired, fast-talking, and full of high energy, and then other entirely different times where I feel heavy, sad, and empty.

Even though my mood felt completely stable and neutral, I still heard the whispering at night and remained convinced my husband's trips were part of a test.\n\nDoctor: During that two to three week period when your mood was neutral, were the whispers and the belief that you were being tested just as strong as they are during your high or low periods?\n\nPatient: Yes, they were absolutely just as strong and felt completely real to me. I start a dozen projects and leave them unfinished, let bills pile up, and miss appointments, which has really strained my relationships with my family. It's even caused me to withdraw from my life and stop going to my book club.\n\nDoctor: During those times when you feel wired and high-energy, does that feeling typically last continuously for at least a week, or is it usually shorter, lasting only a few days?\n\nPatient: I'm not entirely sure of the exact number of days because I tend to lose track of time when I'm in those wired phases. Subtypes: Reduced Sleep Duration, Loss of Sleep Need, Indifference to Insomnia\n[S037] Pressured_Speech: More talkative than usual or pressure to keep talking.  Subtypes: Talkativeness, Rapid Speech, Pressured Speech\n[S038] Racing_Thoughts: Flight of ideas or subjective experience that thoughts are racing.  Subtypes: Racing Thoughts, Flooding of Thoughts, Inability to Control Thoughts\n[S039] Manic_Distractibility: Attention is too easily drawn to unimportant or irrelevant external stimuli.  Subtypes: Responsiveness to Extraneous Stimuli, Frequent Task Switching, Inability to Sustain Attention\n[S040] Increased_Goal_Activity: Increase in goal-directed activity (at work, school, socially, or sexually) or purposeless psychomotor agitation.  Subtypes: Occupational Hyperactivity, Social Hyperactivity, Hypersexuality, Psychomotor Agitation\n[S041] Painful_Consequence_Behavior: Excessive involvement in activities that have a high potential for painful consequences.  Subtypes: Unrestrained Buying Sprees, Foolish Business Investments, Sexual Indiscretions, Impulsive Risky Behavior\n[S042] Low_Self_Esteem: A persistent tendency to evaluate oneself as incompetent or worthless; chronically low self-evaluation.  Subtypes: Feelings of Worthlessness, Self-Criticism, Reduced Self-Efficacy, Chronic Sense of Inferiority, Lack of Self-Assurance\n[S043] Restrictive_Intake: Persistent restriction of energy intake relative to requirements, leading to significantly low body weight. Subtypes: Distorted Body Image, Self-Worth Dependent on Weight\n[S047] Compensatory_Behavior_NonPurging: Recurrent inappropriate compensatory behaviors to prevent weight gain without purging (direct removal of food).  Subtypes: Fasting, Excessive Exercise\n[S048] Compensatory_Behavior_Purging: Recurrent inappropriate compensatory behaviors involving the expulsion of food or fluids to prevent weight gain.  Subtypes: Self-Induced Vomiting, Misuse of Medications\n[S049] Eating_Large_Amount: Eating, in a discrete period of time, an amount of food that is definitely larger than what most individuals would eat in a similar period of time under similar circumstances.\n[S050] Loss_of_Control: A sense of lack of control over eating during the episode (e.g., a feeling that one cannot stop eating or control what or how much one is eating).\n[S051] Rapid_Eating: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n[S052] Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n[S053] Eating_Without_Hunger: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n[S054] Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n[S055] Negative_Affect_After_Binge: Feeling disgusted with oneself, depressed, or very guilty afterward.\n[S084] Low_Weight_Severity_Non_Recognition: Persistent lack of recognition of the seriousness of the current low body weight.  Subtypes: Denial of Medical Risk, Disregard of Symptoms, Dismissal of Concerns\n[S056] Excessive_Anxiety_Worry: Excessive anxiety and worry (expectant apprehension) occurring persistently about a number of events or activities.  Subtypes: Chronic Worry, Generalized Worry, Catastrophizing, Intolerance of Uncertainty\n[S057] Uncontrollable_Worry: The subjective experience of finding it difficult to stop or control the worry.  Subtypes: Failure to Control, Intrusive Worry, Chaining of Worry\n[S058] Restlessness_Tension: Restlessness, or feeling keyed up or on edge. Subtypes: Auditory Hallucinations, Visual Hallucinations, Tactile Hallucinations, Olfactory Hallucinations, Gustatory Hallucinations\n[S021] Disorganized_Thinking_Speech: Symptoms where speech or thought appears scattered and difficult to follow due to a lack of logical or consistent flow.  Subtypes: Derailment or Loose Associations, Tangentiality, Incoherence or Word Salad, Neologisms, Clang Associations\n[S022] Disorganized_Abnormal_Behavior: A state where behavior and motor control are severely disturbed, appearing as movements or reactions inappropriate to the situation.  Subtypes: Childlike Silliness, Stupor, Catalepsy, Waxy Flexibility, Mutism, Negativism, Posturing, Mannerism, Stereotypy, Agitation, Grimacing, Echolalia, Echopraxia\n[S023] Negative_Symptoms: A state where emotions, motivation, speech, or social interest that should normally be present are diminished or lost.  Subtypes: Diminished Emotional Expression, Avolition, Alogia, Anhedonia, Asociality\n[S024] Depressed_Mood: A state where a sad, empty, or hopeless mood persists.  Subtypes: Depressed Mood, Tearfulness, Sadness, Emptiness, Emotional Numbness\n[S025] Loss_of_Interest: A state where interest or pleasure in previously enjoyed activities is markedly diminished.  Subtypes: Loss of Interest, Social Withdrawal, Loss of Motivation, Anhedonia, Indifference to Achievement\n[S026] Weight_Appetite_Change: Significant increase or decrease in appetite or weight.  Subtypes: Significant Weight Loss, Weight Gain, Decrease in Appetite, Increase in Appetite\n[S027] Sleep_Disturbance: A state where there is a significant change in the quantity or quality of sleep.  Subtypes: Initial Insomnia, Middle Insomnia, Early Morning Awakening, Hypersomnia, Nightmare Disorder, Non-Restorative Sleep\n[S028] Psychomotor_Changes: Observable increase or decrease in psychomotor activity.  Subtypes: Psychomotor Agitation, Psychomotor Retardation (Motor), Psychomotor Retardation (Speech)\n[S029] Fatigue: Persistent fatigue that is not relieved by rest and a marked lack of energy.  Subtypes: Chronic Fatigue, Physical Fatigue, Mental Fatigue, Loss of Energy\n[S030] Worthlessness_Guilt: Feelings of worthlessness or excessive or inappropriate guilt (which may be delusional; not merely self-reproach or guilt about being sick).  Subtypes: Feelings of Worthlessness, Ruminative Guilt, Excessive or Inappropriate Guilt, Delusional Guilt\n[S031] Diminished_Thinking: Diminished ability to think or concentrate, or indecisiveness.  Subtypes: Reduced Concentration, Indecisiveness, Subjective Memory Complaints, Clouded Thinking\n[S032] Death_Suicide: Recurrent thoughts of death, recurrent suicidal ideation, or a suicide attempt. Subtypes: Future Hopelessness, Belief in Impossibility of Improvement, Helpless Resignation, Chronic Pessimism\n[S033] Elevated_Irritable_Mood: A distinct period of abnormally and persistently elevated, expansive, or irritable mood.  Subtypes: Elevated Mood, Irritable Mood, Mood Lability\n[S034] Increased_Activity: Abnormally and persistently increased goal-directed activity or energy.  Subtypes: Increased Energy, Hyperactivity, Psychomotor Agitation\n[S035] Inflated_Self_Esteem: Inflated self-esteem or grandiosity (may range from uncritical self-confidence to delusional proportions).  Subtypes: Inflated Self-Confidence, Overestimation of Abilities, Grandiose Delusions\n[S036] Decreased_Sleep: Decreased need for sleep (e.g., feels rested after only 3 hours of sleep).  Subtypes: Reduced Sleep Duration, Loss of Sleep Need, Indifference to Insomnia\n[S037] Pressured_Speech: More talkative than usual or pressure to keep talking.  Subtypes: Talkativeness, Rapid Speech, Pressured Speech\n[S038] Racing_Thoughts: Flight of ideas or subjective experience that thoughts are racing.  Subtypes: Racing Thoughts, Flooding of Thoughts, Inability to Control Thoughts\n[S039] Manic_Distractibility: Attention is too easily drawn to unimportant or irrelevant external stimuli.  Subtypes: Responsiveness to Extraneous Stimuli, Frequent Task Switching, Inability to Sustain Attention\n[S040] Increased_Goal_Activity: Increase in goal-directed activity (at work, school, socially, or sexually) or purposeless psychomotor agitation.  Subtypes: Occupational Hyperactivity, Social Hyperactivity, Hypersexuality, Psychomotor Agitation\n[S041] Painful_Consequence_Behavior: Excessive involvement in activities that have a high potential for painful consequences.  Subtypes: Unrestrained Buying Sprees, Foolish Business Investments, Sexual Indiscretions, Impulsive Risky Behavior\n[S042] Low_Self_Esteem: A persistent tendency to evaluate oneself as incompetent or worthless; chronically low self-evaluation.  Subtypes: Feelings of Worthlessness, Self-Criticism, Reduced Self-Efficacy, Chronic Sense of Inferiority, Lack of Self-Assurance\n[S043] Restrictive_Intake: Persistent restriction of energy intake relative to requirements, leading to significantly low body weight.  Subtypes: Eating Very Small Amounts, Skipping Meals, Exclusion of Specific Food Groups, Strict Calorie Restriction\n[S044] Low_Weight: A body weight that is significantly below the minimum level of health for age, sex, developmental trajectory, and physical health.  Subtypes: Continuous Weight Loss, Insistence on Low Weight\n[S045] Fear_of_Weight_Gain: Intense fear of gaining weight or of becoming fat, even though at a significantly low weight.  Subtypes: Fear of Weight Gain, Fear of Specific Foods, Obsession with Weight Gain\n[S046] Body_Image_Weight_Shape_Disturbance: Undue influence of body weight or shape on self-evaluation.  Subtypes: Distorted Body Image, Self-Worth Dependent on Weight\n[S047] Compensatory_Behavior_NonPurging: Recurrent inappropriate compensatory behaviors to prevent weight gain without purging (direct removal of food).  Subtypes: Fasting, Excessive Exercise\n[S048] Compensatory_Behavior_Purging: Recurrent inappropriate compensatory behaviors involving the expulsion of food or fluids to prevent weight gain.  Subtypes: Self-Induced Vomiting, Misuse of Medications\n[S049] Eating_Large_Amount: Eating, in a discrete period of time, an amount of food that is definitely larger than what most individuals would eat in a similar period of time under similar circumstances.\n[S050] Loss_of_Control: A sense of lack of control over eating during the episode (e.g., a feeling that one cannot stop eating or control what or how much one is eating).\n[S051] Rapid_Eating: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n[S052] Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n[S053] Eating_Without_Hunger: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n[S054] Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n[S055] Negative_Affect_After_Binge: Feeling disgusted with oneself, depressed, or very guilty afterward.\n[S084] Low_Weight_Severity_Non_Recognition: Persistent lack of recognition of the seriousness of the current low body weight.  Subtypes: Denial of Medical Risk, Disregard of Symptoms, Dismissal of Concerns\n[S056] Excessive_Anxiety_Worry: Excessive anxiety and worry (expectant apprehension) occurring persistently about a number of events or activities.  Subtypes: Chronic Worry, Generalized Worry, Catastrophizing, Intolerance of Uncertainty\n[S057] Uncontrollable_Worry: The subjective experience of finding it difficult to stop or control the worry.  Subtypes: Failure to Control, Intrusive Worry, Chaining of Worry\n[S058] Restlessness_Tension: Restlessness, or feeling keyed up or on edge.  Subtypes: Feeling Keyed Up, Physical Tension, Nervousness\n[S059] Concentration_Difficulty: Difficulty concentrating or mind going blank.  Subtypes: Interference by Worry, Inability to Sustain Attention, Mind Going Blank\n[S060] Irritability: A state of reacting sensitively or sharply to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Low Frustration Tolerance, Snappiness, Exhausted Patience\n[S061] Muscle_Tension: A state where muscles are continuously tense, stiff, and accompanied by discomfort.  Subtypes: Neck and Shoulder Tension, Jaw Tension, Generalized Muscle Aches, Chronic Stiffness\n[S062] Specific_Phobic_Fear: Marked fear or anxiety about a specific object or situation that is out of proportion to the actual danger. (Note: In children, may be expressed by crying, tantrums, freezing, or clinging).  Subtypes: Animal Type, Natural Environment Type, Blood-Injection-Injury Type, Situational Type, Other Type\n[S063] Immediate_Fear/Anxiety_Response: The phobic object or situation almost always provokes immediate fear or anxiety.  Subtypes: Anticipatory Anxiety, Immediate Panic Response, Physiological Panic Symptoms, Freezing\n[S064] Phobic_Avoidance_Endure: The phobic object or situation is actively avoided or endured with intense fear or anxiety. Subtypes: Contamination Obsessions, Pathological Doubt, Symmetry and Exactness Obsessions, Forbidden or Taboo Thoughts, Somatic Obsessions\n[S066] Compulsions: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to an obsession or to prevent anxiety/distress; clearly excessive or not realistically connected.  Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others.  Subtypes: Skin Defects, Facial Features, Asymmetry, Muscle Dysmorphia, Specific Body Parts\n[S068] Appearance_Related_Repetitive_Behaviors: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to appearance concerns. Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Internal Avoidance, External Avoidance\n[S071] Trauma_Related_Amnesia: Inability to remember an important aspect of the traumatic event (typically due to dissociative amnesia and not to other factors like head injury or alcohol).  Subtypes: Fragmented Memory, Generalized Amnesia\n[S072] Negative_Beliefs: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world.  Subtypes: Negative Self-Beliefs, Negative Beliefs about Others, Negative Worldview\n[S073] Distorted_Cognitions_With_Blame: Persistent, distorted cognitions about the cause or consequences of the traumatic event that lead the individual to blame himself/herself or others.  Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others. Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health. Subtypes: Denial of Medical Risk, Disregard of Symptoms, Dismissal of Concerns\n[S056] Excessive_Anxiety_Worry: Excessive anxiety and worry (expectant apprehension) occurring persistently about a number of events or activities. Subtypes: Fragmented Memory, Generalized Amnesia\n[S072] Negative_Beliefs: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world. Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health.
